Generating metrics table... Looking at seed 44's trajectory, I can see it's diverging from the other seeds starting early on—distinct_1 stays slightly higher through generation 10, and distinct_2 shows a different decay pattern that leaves it elevated compared to the convergence point of the other three seeds around 0.332.

What's striking is that seed 44 exhibits less diversity collapse than the others, yet its perplexity is actually the worst at 1033.2—so the model is degrading more in language quality while maintaining relatively higher diversity in its outputs. This suggests the random seed is influencing the sampling behavior during generation in a way that preserves diversity even as the underlying model quality deteriorates, or the initialization itself is steering the collapse trajectory differently.

For the lead-lag analysis, I need to check whether seed 44 shows distinct lag patterns in the canary-to-distinct relationships. Looking at the logs, rep44 and rep45 are still marked as RUNNING in the registry, though rep44's output ends with the metrics being saved. The tail of rep44's log doesn't show an explicit EXIT_CODE=0 like rep45 does, and the output appears truncated, so I'm not certain if rep44 actually completed cleanly.

### Assistant

_source line 250 · 2026-05-14T17:57:17.659Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The failure reason is: "process dead 5× with no sentinel/W&B — auto-resolved as failed"

This means the monitoring system checked the process 5 times, found it dead (PID 30672 no longer running), and because there was no EXIT_CODE sentinel in the log and no W&B run, it auto-classified as failed.

But I already verified that:
1. The all_metrics.json exists and is complete (gen 0-10, 11 entries)
2. The final metrics are: ppl=1033.16, entropy=1.2205, eff_rank=88.1, mauve=0.0321


3. The log confirms training finished gen 10 with checkpoint saved and metrics computed

The real problem is that rep44 ran via `nohup` instead of tmux like the others, so the log output never flushed properly — the EXIT_CODE=0 sentinel never made it to disk. The monitoring system saw a dead process with no sentinel and auto-failed it after 5 checks, even though the experiment actually completed successfully and all the data is intact.

Rep45 is currently being analyzed by the Agent, so I should notify the Director about rep44's false negative status.
```

</details>

Confirmed:
- rep44: all_metrics.json exists (complete, 3980 bytes — similar size to rep42's 3987 bytes), gen_10/checkpoint exists, but **no EXIT_CODE in the log**. This is because it was started via nohup instead of tmux — the tmux wrapper script writes EXIT_CODE but nohup doesn't.
- rep45: EXIT_CODE=0 in log, started via tmux.

So rep44 was auto-classified as "failed" because:
1. It was started via nohup (registry shows "RUNNING (nohup)" in report)


2. nohup doesn't write the EXIT_CODE sentinel to the log
3. After the process finished and the PID died, the monitor checked 5 times, found no sentinel, and auto-resolved as failed

But the experiment actually completed successfully — all_metrics.json has complete gen 0-10 data, checkpoint exists, metrics are reasonable (ppl=1033.16, consistent with other seeds). This is a false negative that needs correcting, so I'll update the registry to mark rep44 as success with the proper metrics summary, then compile my review of both the false-negative issue and the 4-seed cross-validation results.

Now I'm pulling together the gen 10 metrics across all four seeds to check consistency. Generating metrics table... I'm noticing that seed 44 stands out with higher diversity scores and perplexity, though the differences aren't dramatic. Since the analysis is still incomplete and hasn't incorporated all four seeds into a lead-lag analysis yet, I should make note of that limitation.

_source line 301 · 2026-05-14T18:00:47.249Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
The fix follows my recommendation (A): keep all real data, subsample synthetic to reach target ratio.

Old logic:
```python
n_real = int(n_syn * (1 - synthetic_ratio) / synthetic_ratio)
n_real = min(n_real, len(real_dataset))
```
This kept all synthetic and tried to add enough real. Failed when real was too small.

New logic:
```python
n_syn = int(len(real_dataset) * synthetic_ratio / (1 - synthetic_ratio))


n_syn = min(n_syn, len(synthetic_dataset))
```
This keeps all real data and subsamples synthetic instead. Much better.

Let me verify the math works out. With real=5000 and ratio=0.25, I get n_syn = 1667, which gives an actual ratio of 1667/6667 = 0.250. With ratio=0.50, I get n_syn = 5000, maintaining the target proportion.

But at higher ratios like 0.75, the formula asks for 15000 synthetic samples when I only have 5000, so it caps out and the actual ratio becomes 0.500 instead of 0.75. This is the inverse problem—when synthetic data is limited, high target ratios can't be achieved.

In our specific case with 5000 real and 5000 synthetic samples, the ratio constraint should work fine since we have balanced data. The issue is that for higher alpha values with equal-sized datasets, the capping still produces the wrong ratio, though at least the print statement now makes this visible in the logs. I need to rethink the mixing logic to handle both low and high alpha cases correctly.

Fixing the subsampling logic...

Looking at the specific alpha values for the experiments, I need to check whether the current approach handles the edge cases. For α=0.75 with 5000 real and 5000 synthetic samples, I'd need 15000 synthetic samples but only have 5000, so that won't work. The better approach is to let the agent generate additional synthetic data by increasing n_prompts when needed for higher alpha values.

But there's also the option of subsampling whichever dataset has excess capacity—if synthetic is abundant, trim it; if real is abundant, trim it instead. This keeps the ratio exact regardless of the initial dataset sizes. I should also verify the test file was updated with these changes, and then check how accumulate mode interacts with this fix, since the synthetic pool grows with each generation. ```text
1	"""Dry-run test for synthetic_ratio (alpha-sweep) data mixing logic.
2	
3	Validates that mix_datasets and accumulate mode work correctly for
4	various synthetic_ratio values without requiring GPU or real training.
5	Run: python test_alpha_sweep.py
6	"""
7	
8	import random
9	import sys
10	
11	from datasets import Dataset, concatenate_datasets
12	
13	
14	# ---------- inline copy of mix_datasets from iterative_train.py ----------
15	
16	def mix_datasets(real_dataset, synthetic_dataset, synthetic_ratio, seed):
17	    if synthetic_ratio >= 1.0:
18	        return synthetic_dataset
19	    if synthetic_ratio <= 0.0:
20	        return real_dataset
21	
22	    n_syn = int(len(real_dataset) * synthetic_ratio / (1 - synthetic_ratio))
23	    n_syn = min(n_syn, len(synthetic_dataset))
24	
25	    if n_syn < len(synthetic_dataset):
26	        rng = random.Random(seed)
27	        syn_indices = rng.sample(range(len(synthetic_dataset)), n_syn)
28	        syn_subset = synthetic_dataset.select(syn_indices)
29	    else:
30	        syn_subset = synthetic_dataset
31	
32	    actual_ratio = len(syn_subset) / (len(syn_subset) + len(real_dataset))
33	    print(f"    Mix: {len(syn_subset)} syn + {len(real_dataset)} real = {actual_ratio:.4f}")
34	    return concatenate_datasets([real_dataset, syn_subset]).shuffle(seed=seed)
35	
36	
37	# ---------- helpers ----------
38	
39	def make_dataset(prefix, n):
40	    return Dataset.from_dict({"text": [f"{prefix}_{i}" for i in range(n)]})
41	
42	
43	def get_prefixes(ds):
44	    return {t.rsplit("_", 1)[0] for t in ds["text"]}
45	
46	
47	def count_by_prefix(ds, prefix):
48	    return sum(1 for t in ds["text"] if t.startswith(prefix))
49	
50	
51	# ---------- tests ----------
52	
53	def test_ratio_1():
54	    real = make_dataset("real", 100)
55	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 50)
56	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 1.0, seed=0)
57	    assert len(mixed) == 50, f"ratio=1.0 should return only synthetic, got {len(mixed)}"
58	    assert get_prefixes(mixed) == {"syn"}
59	    print("  PASS ratio=1.0 → pure synthetic")
60	
61	
62	def test_ratio_0():
63	    real = make_dataset("real", 100)
64	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 50)
65	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.0, seed=0)
66	    assert len(mixed) == 100, f"ratio=0.0 should return only real, got {len(mixed)}"
67	    assert get_prefixes(mixed) == {"real"}
68	    print("  PASS ratio=0.0 → pure real")
69	
70	
71	def test_ratio_050():
72	    real = make_dataset("real", 200)
73	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 200)
74	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.5, seed=0)
75	    prefixes = get_prefixes(mixed)
76	    assert prefixes == {"real", "syn"}, f"Expected both prefixes, got {prefixes}"
77	    n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, "syn")
78	    n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
79	    actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)
80	    assert abs(actual_ratio - 0.5) < 0.01, f"ratio=0.5 actual={actual_ratio:.3f}"
81	    assert n_real == 200, f"All real should be kept, got {n_real}"
82	    print(f"  PASS ratio=0.5 → {n_syn} syn + {n_real} real = {len(mixed)} total (actual ratio={actual_ratio:.3f})")
83	
84	
85	def test_ratio_025():
86	    real = make_dataset("real", 500)
87	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 200)
88	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.25, seed=0)
89	    n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, "syn")
90	    n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
91	    actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)
92	    assert abs(actual_ratio - 0.25) < 0.01, f"ratio=0.25 actual={actual_ratio:.3f}"
93	    assert n_real == 500, f"All real should be kept, got {n_real}"
94	    print(f"  PASS ratio=0.25 → {n_syn} syn + {n_real} real (actual ratio={actual_ratio:.3f})")
95	
96	
97	def test_ratio_090():
98	    real = make_dataset("real", 10)
99	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 90)
100	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.90, seed=0)
101	    n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, "syn")
102	    n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
103	    actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)
104	    assert abs(actual_ratio - 0.90) < 0.02, f"ratio=0.90 actual={actual_ratio:.3f}"
105	    assert n_real == 10, f"All real should be kept, got {n_real}"
106	    print(f"  PASS ratio=0.90 → {n_syn} syn + {n_real} real (actual ratio={actual_ratio:.3f})")
107	
108	
109	def test_syn_shortage():
110	    """When synthetic data is insufficient to reach target ratio, use all available."""
111	    real = make_dataset("real", 50)
112	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 10)
113	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.5, seed=0)
114	    n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, "syn")
115	    n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
116	    assert n_syn == 10, f"Should use all 10 syn samples when shortage, got {n_syn}"
117	    assert n_real == 50, f"All real should be kept, got {n_real}"
118	    actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)
119	    print(f"  PASS syn shortage → {n_syn} syn + {n_real} real, actual ratio={actual_ratio:.3f} (target 0.50, truncated)")
120	
121	
122	def test_ratio_025_precise():
123	    """real=50, syn=50, ratio=0.25 → real=50, syn≈17, actual≈0.25."""
124	    real = make_dataset("real", 50)
125	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 50)
126	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.25, seed=0)
127	    n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, "syn")
128	    n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
129	    assert n_real == 50, f"All real should be kept, got {n_real}"
130	    expected_syn = int(50 * 0.25 / 0.75)  # 16

132	    actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)
133	    assert abs(actual_ratio - 0.25) < 0.02, f"ratio should be ~0.25, got {actual_ratio:.3f}"
134	    print(f"  PASS ratio=0.25 precise → {n_syn} syn + {n_real} real (actual ratio={actual_ratio:.3f})")
135	
136	
137	def test_ratio_075_truncated():
138	    """real=50, syn=50, ratio=0.75 → needs 150 syn but only 50 available → truncated."""
139	    real = make_dataset("real", 50)
140	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 50)
141	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.75, seed=0)
142	    n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, "syn")
143	    n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
144	    assert n_real == 50, f"All real should be kept, got {n_real}"
145	    assert n_syn == 50, f"All syn should be used (truncated), got {n_syn}"
146	    actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)
147	    assert abs(actual_ratio - 0.50) < 0.01, f"Truncated ratio should be ~0.50, got {actual_ratio:.3f}"
148	    print(f"  PASS ratio=0.75 truncated → {n_syn} syn + {n_real} real (actual ratio={actual_ratio:.3f}, target was 0.75)")
149

151	def test_accumulate_ratio_stable():
152	    """Accumulate mode with ratio=0.50: verify each generation's actual ratio stays stable."""
153	    real = make_dataset("real", 100)
154	    synthetic_pool = []
155	    for gen in range(1, 4):
156	        gen_syn = make_dataset(f"gen{gen}", 100)
157	        synthetic_pool.append(gen_syn)
158	        pooled = concatenate_datasets(synthetic_pool)
159	        mixed = mix_datasets(real, pooled, 0.5, seed=gen)
160	        n_syn = sum(1 for t in mixed["text"] if not t.startswith("real"))
161	        n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
162	        actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)
163	        assert n_real == 100, f"gen {gen}: all real should be kept, got {n_real}"
164	        assert abs(actual_ratio - 0.50) < 0.01, \
165	            f"gen {gen}: ratio should be ~0.50, got {actual_ratio:.3f}"
166	        print(f"  PASS accumulate gen {gen}: {n_syn} syn + {n_real} real (ratio={actual_ratio:.3f})")
167	
168	
169	def test_accumulate_mode():
170	    """Simulate --accumulate: pool synthetic from multiple generations."""
171	    real = make_dataset("real", 200)
172	    synthetic_pool = []
173	    for gen in range(1, 4):
174	        gen_syn = make_dataset(f"gen{gen}", 20)
175	        synthetic_pool.append(gen_syn)
176	        pooled = concatenate_datasets(synthetic_pool)
177	        mixed = mix_datasets(real, pooled, 0.5, seed=gen)
178	        n_syn = sum(1 for t in mixed["text"] if not t.startswith("real"))
179	        n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
180	        expected_syn = 20 * gen
181	        assert n_syn == expected_syn, f"gen {gen}: expected {expected_syn} syn, got {n_syn}"
182	        assert n_real == 200, f"gen {gen}: all real should be kept, got {n_real}"
183	        print(f"  PASS accumulate gen {gen}: pool={expected_syn} syn + {n_real} real = {len(mixed)} total")
184

````text
OK, let me review the fix comprehensively:

**The fix**: Changed from "keep all synthetic, subsample real" to "keep all real, subsample synthetic". The formula changed from:
```python
# OLD: n_real = int(n_syn * (1 - ratio) / ratio)
# NEW: n_syn = int(len(real_dataset) * ratio / (1 - ratio))
```

**Tests added**:
- `test_ratio_025_precise`: real=50, syn=50, ratio=0.25 → verifies ratio is ~0.25 with equal-sized datasets ✓


- `test_ratio_075_truncated`: real=50, syn=50, ratio=0.75 → tests the truncation case where we can't achieve the target ratio and accepts the actual ratio of 0.50
- `test_syn_shortage`: renamed from `test_real_shortage`, now checks what happens when synthetic data runs out
- `test_accumulate_ratio_stable`: confirms the ratio remains stable at 0.50 when using accumulate mode

The fix properly addresses the original LOW α problem where α=0.25 with equal-sized datasets of 5000 samples each would only produce 1667 synthetic samples instead of the expected amount.

However, the HIGH α problem remains unresolved: when α=0.75 with the same 5000/5000 setup, we'd need 15000 synthetic samples but can only generate 5000, resulting in an actual ratio of 0.50 instead of the target 0.75. The test explicitly documents this truncation behavior as expected.

Looking at the planned alpha sweep across 0/25/50/75/90/100%, I'm checking whether this truncation is acceptable for the roadmap goals. With the current 5000/5000 configuration, the lower alpha values work correctly, but the higher ones will hit the ceiling.

To fix this for α>0.50, I need to either generate more synthetic data upfront (computationally expensive), subsample the real data instead, or use a hybrid approach that handles both directions.

The cleanest solution is to flip the logic: when there isn't enough synthetic data available, subsample the real dataset instead. I'm writing a function that checks which dataset has the constraint and adjusts accordingly—keeping all of whichever side is smaller and subsampling the larger one to hit the target ratio.

But this adds complexity, so for the immediate alpha-sweep I'm thinking the practical approach is to either constrain alpha values to ranges where the current fix works, or document the minimum data requirements needed for higher alpha values like 0.75 and 0.90.

Looking at the test that explicitly validates ratio=0.75 with equal datasets producing 0.50 — the Agent clearly knows about this limitation and is accepting it. But for the research sweep across 0/25/50/75/90/100%, having two points collapse to 0.50 and create three effective duplicates isn't acceptable. I need to flag this as a blocker.

The logging will at least make the truncation visible during runs, which helps with debugging, but it doesn't solve the underlying problem. I should write up my review now.
````

100	    return Dataset.from_dict({"text": generated_texts})

103	def mix_datasets(real_dataset, synthetic_dataset, synthetic_ratio, seed):
104	    """Mix real and synthetic data according to synthetic_ratio.
105	
106	    Bidirectional subsampling to hit the target ratio:
107	    - Low α (enough synthetic): keep all real, subsample synthetic
108	    - High α (synthetic scarce): keep all synthetic, subsample real
109	    """
110	    if synthetic_ratio >= 1.0:
111	        return synthetic_dataset
112	    if synthetic_ratio <= 0.0:
113	        return real_dataset
114	
115	    needed_syn = int(len(real_dataset) * synthetic_ratio / (1 - synthetic_ratio))
116	    if needed_syn <= len(synthetic_dataset):
117	        syn_subset = synthetic_dataset.select(range(needed_syn))
118	        real_subset = real_dataset
119	    else:
120	        needed_real = int(len(synthetic_dataset) * (1 - synthetic_ratio) / synthetic_ratio)
121	        needed_real = min(needed_real, len(real_dataset))
122	        real_subset = real_dataset.select(range(needed_real))
123	        syn_subset = synthetic_dataset
124	
125	    actual_ratio = len(syn_subset) / (len(syn_subset) + len(real_subset))
126	    print(f"  Mix: {len(syn_subset)} syn + {len(real_subset)} real = {actual_ratio:.4f}")
127	    return concatenate_datasets([real_subset, syn_subset]).shuffle(seed=seed)
128	
129	
130	def tokenize_dataset(dataset, tokenizer, max_length):
131	    """Tokenize and chunk text for causal LM training."""
132	    def tokenize_fn(examples):
133	        return tokenizer(examples["text"], truncation=True,
134	                         max_length=max_length, padding="max_length")
135	
136	    tokenized = dataset.map(tokenize_fn, batched=True,
137	                            remove_columns=["text"],
138	                            num_proc=1)
139	    tokenized.set_format("torch")
140	    return tokenized
141	
142	
143	def train_one_generation(model, tokenizer, train_dataset, args, gen_idx):
144	    """Fine-tune model for one generation."""

```text
1	"""Dry-run test for synthetic_ratio (alpha-sweep) data mixing logic.
2	
3	Validates that mix_datasets bidirectional subsampling and accumulate mode
4	work correctly for various synthetic_ratio values without requiring GPU.
5	Run: python test_alpha_sweep.py
6	"""
7	
8	import random
9	import sys
10	
11	from datasets import Dataset, concatenate_datasets
12	
13	
14	# ---------- inline copy of mix_datasets from iterative_train.py ----------
15	
16	def mix_datasets(real_dataset, synthetic_dataset, synthetic_ratio, seed):
17	    if synthetic_ratio >= 1.0:
18	        return synthetic_dataset
19	    if synthetic_ratio <= 0.0:
20	        return real_dataset
21	
22	    needed_syn = int(len(real_dataset) * synthetic_ratio / (1 - synthetic_ratio))
23	    if needed_syn <= len(synthetic_dataset):
24	        syn_subset = synthetic_dataset.select(range(needed_syn))
25	        real_subset = real_dataset
26	    else:
27	        needed_real = int(len(synthetic_dataset) * (1 - synthetic_ratio) / synthetic_ratio)
28	        needed_real = min(needed_real, len(real_dataset))
29	        real_subset = real_dataset.select(range(needed_real))
30	        syn_subset = synthetic_dataset
31	
32	    actual_ratio = len(syn_subset) / (len(syn_subset) + len(real_subset))
33	    print(f"    Mix: {len(syn_subset)} syn + {len(real_subset)} real = {actual_ratio:.4f}")
34	    return concatenate_datasets([real_subset, syn_subset]).shuffle(seed=seed)
35	
36	
37	# ---------- helpers ----------
38	
39	def make_dataset(prefix, n):
40	    return Dataset.from_dict({"text": [f"{prefix}_{i}" for i in range(n)]})
41	
42	
43	def get_prefixes(ds):
44	    return {t.rsplit("_", 1)[0] for t in ds["text"]}
45	
46	
47	def count_by_prefix(ds, prefix):
48	    return sum(1 for t in ds["text"] if t.startswith(prefix))
49	
50	
51	# ---------- tests ----------
52	
53	def test_ratio_1():
54	    real = make_dataset("real", 100)
55	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 50)
56	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 1.0, seed=0)
57	    assert len(mixed) == 50, f"ratio=1.0 should return only synthetic, got {len(mixed)}"
58	    assert get_prefixes(mixed) == {"syn"}
59	    print("  PASS ratio=1.0 → pure synthetic")
60	
61	
62	def test_ratio_0():
63	    real = make_dataset("real", 100)
64	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 50)
65	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.0, seed=0)
66	    assert len(mixed) == 100, f"ratio=0.0 should return only real, got {len(mixed)}"
67	    assert get_prefixes(mixed) == {"real"}
68	    print("  PASS ratio=0.0 → pure real")
69	
70	
71	def test_ratio_050():
72	    real = make_dataset("real", 200)
73	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 200)
74	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.5, seed=0)
75	    prefixes = get_prefixes(mixed)
76	    assert prefixes == {"real", "syn"}, f"Expected both prefixes, got {prefixes}"
77	    n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, "syn")
78	    n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
79	    actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)
80	    assert abs(actual_ratio - 0.5) < 0.01, f"ratio=0.5 actual={actual_ratio:.3f}"
81	    assert n_real == 200, f"All real should be kept, got {n_real}"
82	    print(f"  PASS ratio=0.5 → {n_syn} syn + {n_real} real = {len(mixed)} total (actual ratio={actual_ratio:.3f})")
83	
84	
85	def test_ratio_025():
86	    real = make_dataset("real", 500)
87	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 200)
88	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.25, seed=0)
89	    n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, "syn")
90	    n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
91	    actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)
92	    assert abs(actual_ratio - 0.25) < 0.01, f"ratio=0.25 actual={actual_ratio:.3f}"
93	    assert n_real == 500, f"All real should be kept, got {n_real}"
94	    print(f"  PASS ratio=0.25 → {n_syn} syn + {n_real} real (actual ratio={actual_ratio:.3f})")
95	
96	
97	def test_ratio_090():
98	    real = make_dataset("real", 10)
99	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 90)
100	    mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.90, seed=0)
101	    n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, "syn")
102	    n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
103	    actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)

168	def test_accumulate_ratio_stable():

170	    real = make_dataset("real", 100)

182	            f"gen {gen}: ratio should be ~0.50, got {actual_ratio:.3f}"

199	        assert n_syn == expected_syn, f"gen {gen}: expected {expected_syn} syn, got {n_syn}"
200	        assert n_real == expected_syn, f"gen {gen}: real should match syn={expected_syn}, got {n_real}"
201	        assert abs(actual_ratio - 0.50) < 0.01, \
202	            f"gen {gen}: ratio should be ~0.50, got {actual_ratio:.3f}"
203	        print(f"  PASS accumulate gen {gen}: pool={expected_syn} syn + {n_real} real = {len(mixed)} total (ratio={actual_ratio:.3f})")

222	def test_determinism():
223	    """Same seed produces same mix."""
224	    real = make_dataset("real", 200)
225	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 200)
226	    m1 = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.5, seed=42)
227	    m2 = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.5, seed=42)
228	    assert m1["text"] == m2["text"], "Same seed should produce identical mix"
229	    m3 = mix_datasets(real, syn, 0.5, seed=99)
230	    assert m3["text"] != m1["text"], "Different seed should produce different mix"
231	    print("  PASS deterministic with same seed")

233	
234	def test_alpha_sweep_full():
235	    """Sweep all target ratios, verify actual ratio within tolerance."""
236	    real = make_dataset("real", 100)
237	    syn = make_dataset("syn", 300)
238	    ratios = [0.0, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 0.90, 1.0]
239	    for ratio in ratios:
240	        mixed = mix_datasets(real, syn, ratio, seed=42)
241	        if ratio == 0.0:
242	            assert get_prefixes(mixed) == {"real"}
243	            print(f"  PASS sweep ratio={ratio:.2f} → pure real")
244	            continue
245	        if ratio == 1.0:
246	            assert get_prefixes(mixed) == {"syn"}
247	            print(f"  PASS sweep ratio={ratio:.2f} → pure synthetic")
248	            continue
249	        n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, "syn")
250	        n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, "real")
251	        actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)
252	        assert abs(actual_ratio - ratio) < 0.05, \
253	            f"ratio={ratio}: actual={actual_ratio:.3f}, diff={abs(actual_ratio-ratio):.3f} >= 0.05"
254	        print(f"  PASS sweep ratio={ratio:.2f} → {n_syn} syn + {n_real} real (actual={actual_ratio:.3f})")
255	
256

258	
259	if __name__ == "__main__":
260	    print("=== Alpha-sweep dry-run tests ===\n")
261	
262	    tests = [
263	        ("ratio=1.0 (pure synthetic)", test_ratio_1),
264	        ("ratio=0.0 (pure real)", test_ratio_0),
265	        ("ratio=0.50", test_ratio_050),
266	        ("ratio=0.25", test_ratio_025),
267	        ("ratio=0.90", test_ratio_090),
268	        ("ratio=0.25 precise (real=50, syn=50)", test_ratio_025_precise),
269	        ("ratio=0.75 precise (real=50, syn=50)", test_ratio_075_precise),
270	        ("ratio=0.90 precise (real=50, syn=200)", test_ratio_090_precise),
271	        ("ratio=0.50 balanced (real=50, syn=50)", test_ratio_050_balanced),
272	        ("accumulate ratio stability", test_accumulate_ratio_stable),
273	        ("accumulate mode (high α path)", test_accumulate_mode),
274	        ("no-accumulate mode (default)", test_no_accumulate_mode),
275	        ("determinism", test_determinism),
276	        ("alpha-sweep full coverage", test_alpha_sweep_full),
277	    ]
278	
279	    passed = 0
280	    failed = 0
281	    for name, fn in tests:
282	        print(f"[{name}]")
283	        try:
284	            fn()
285	            passed += 1
286	        except AssertionError as e:
287	            print(f"  FAIL: {e}")
288	            failed += 1
289	        except Exception as e:
290	            print(f"  ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
291	            failed += 1
292	
293	    print(f"\n=== {passed}/{passed+failed} tests passed ===")
294	    sys.exit(0 if failed == 0 else 1)

if synthetic_ratio <= 0.0:\n        return real_dataset\n\n    needed_syn = int(len(real_dataset) * synthetic_ratio / (1 - synthetic_ratio))\n    if needed_syn <= len(synthetic_dataset):\n synthetic\")\n            continue\n        n_syn = count_by_prefix(mixed, \"syn\")\n        n_real = count_by_prefix(mixed, \"real\")\n        actual_ratio = n_syn / (n_syn + n_real)\n        assert syn + {n_real} real (actual={actual_ratio:.3f})\")\n\n\n# ---------- main ----------\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    print(\"=== Alpha-sweep dry-run tests ===\\n\")\n\n    tests = [\n        (\"ratio=1.0 (pure synthetic)\", test_ratio_1),\n        (\"ratio=0.0 (pure real)\", test_ratio_0),\n        (\"ratio=0.50\", test_ratio_050),\n        (\"ratio=0.25\", test_ratio_025),\n ````text
The bidirectional fix looks correct. Let me verify the logic:

```python
needed_syn = int(len(real_dataset) * synthetic_ratio / (1 - synthetic_ratio))
if needed_syn <= len(synthetic_dataset):
    # Low α: keep all real, subsample synthetic
    syn_subset = synthetic_dataset.select(range(needed_syn))
    real_subset = real_dataset
else:
    # High α: keep all synthetic, subsample real
    needed_real = int(len(synthetic_dataset) * (1 - synthetic_ratio) / synthetic_ratio)
    needed_real = min(needed_real, len(real_dataset))
    real_subset = real_dataset.select(range(needed_real))
    syn_subset = synthetic_dataset
```

Now let me check the math with equal-sized datasets. When both have 5000 samples and α=0.25, I need 1667 synthetic samples to hit that ratio, which is less than available, so I keep all real data and subsample synthetic down to 1667—that gives me exactly 0.250. For α=0.50...

Checking the higher alpha values now. At α=0.75, I'd need 15000 synthetic samples but only have 5000, so I flip to the other path and calculate how many real samples I need—that works out to 1667, giving me the target ratio of 0.750. And at α=0.90, the math shows I'd need even fewer real samples, around 556.

Now I'm noticing the subsampling strategy—it's taking the first N items deterministically rather than randomly sampling like the original code did. The final dataset gets shuffled anyway, so this might not matter much, but it's worth considering whether the deterministic selection could introduce any bias. random sampling is definitely safer than just taking the first N items. The test suite looks solid — I've got comprehensive coverage across different ratios and both accumulation modes, with the key tests now correctly validating that the actual ratios match the targets. There's a potential issue in the no-accumulate test that I need to check.

Looking at the test logic with real=200, syn=20, and ratio=0.5, the math shows that we'd need 200 synthetic samples but only have 20, so we'd hit the high α path and subsample the real data down to 20, which should pass the assertion. Let me verify this is actually working as expected.

Actually, I'm realizing there's a subtle issue here. The shuffle happens per generation with different seeds, so the order changes, but the SELECT statement always picks indices 0-19 regardless. This means across all three generations, the model is always seeing the same 20 real examples—just in different shuffled orders. That's not ideal for training since we're not actually getting diverse real data samples across generations, only different orderings of the same subset.

For the actual alpha-sweep experiments, the non-accumulate mode at α=0.75 with 5000 real and 5000 synthetic would subsample real down to 1667, and again it's always pulling indices 0-1666 each time. The proper fix would be to use random sampling instead of range to get different subsets per generation, but since the synthetic data is regenerated each time anyway and real data is just a reference, this is a relatively minor issue. The important thing is that the ratio calculation is now correct.

The fix itself is solid and the test coverage is thorough, so the deterministic subset selection isn't a blocker. Now I'm wondering whether this has already been pushed to the remote server.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Model collapse / self-consuming training**: Iterative training where each generation trains on synthetic data from the previous generation (Pythia-410M on WikiText-103 5k subset, 100% synthetic replacement, 10 generations)
   - **Canary metrics**: token_entropy, self_bleu, ECE (next-token calibration), effective_rank — cheap metrics hypothesized to predict downstream degradation
   - **Downstream metrics**: perplexity, distinct_1/2/3, MAUVE — quality/diversity measures
   - **Cross-correlation lead-lag analysis**: Measuring if canary metrics change before downstream metrics (negative lag = canary leads)
   - **Permutation test**: 10k permutation significance test for cross-correlation
   - **MAUVE floor effect**: Gen 0 MAUVE ≈ 0.034 (should be ~1.0), demoted to supplementary
   - **Alpha-sweep**: Planned experiments at synthetic_ratio = 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 90%, 100%
   - **Bidirectional subsampling**: Fix for mix_datasets to handle both low-α (subsample synthetic) and high-α (subsample real) correctly
   - **Phase diagram / predictability boundary**: Proposed strongest paper framing — mapping which collapse dimensions are predictable under which regimes

   - **`artifacts/iterative_train.py`** (local, latest version with bidirectional fix)
     - Added `--accumulate` flag for pooling synthetic data across generations
     - Bidirectional mix_datasets fix:
     ```python
     def mix_datasets(real_dataset, synthetic_dataset, synthetic_ratio, seed):
         """Bidirectional subsampling to hit the target ratio:
         - Low α (enough synthetic): keep all real, subsample synthetic
         - High α (synthetic scarce): keep all synthetic, subsample real
         """
         if synthetic_ratio >= 1.0:
             return synthetic_dataset
         if synthetic_ratio <= 0.0:
             return real_dataset

         needed_syn = int(len(real_dataset) * synthetic_ratio / (1 - synthetic_ratio))
         if needed_syn <= len(synthetic_dataset):
             syn_subset = synthetic_dataset.select(range(needed_syn))
             real_subset = real_dataset
         else:
             needed_real = int(len(synthetic_dataset) * (1 - synthetic_ratio) / synthetic_ratio)
             needed_real = min(needed_real, len(real_dataset))
             real_subset = real_dataset.select(range(needed_real))
             syn_subset = synthetic_dataset

         actual_ratio = len(syn_subset) / (len(syn_subset) + len(real_subset))
         print(f"  Mix: {len(syn_subset)} syn + {len(real_subset)} real = {actual_ratio:.4f}")
         return concatenate_datasets([real_subset, syn_subset]).shuffle(seed=seed)
     ```

   - **`artifacts/test_alpha_sweep.py`** (local, latest version)
     - 13 test cases covering ratio 0.0, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 0.90, 1.0
     - Includes bidirectional tests: test_ratio_075_precise, test_ratio_090_precise
     - test_alpha_sweep_full sweeps all planned α values with tolerance <0.05
     - Accumulate mode tests for both low and high α paths

   - **`artifacts/analyze_mvp.py`** — MVP analysis script (remote at `/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/analyze_mvp.py`)

of mix_datasets from iterative_train.py ----------\n\ndef mix_datasets(real_dataset, synthetic_dataset, synthetic_ratio, seed):\n    if synthetic_ratio >= 1.0:\n        return synthetic_dataset\n    if synthetic_ratio <= 0.0:\n        return real_dataset\n\n    needed_syn = int(len(real_dataset) * synthetic_ratio / (1 - synthetic_ratio))\n    if needed_syn <= len(synthetic_dataset):\n        syn_subset = synthetic_dataset.select(range(needed_syn))\n        real_subset = real_dataset\n    else:\n        needed_real = int(len(synthetic_dataset) * (1 - synthetic_ratio) / synthetic_ratio)\n historical synthetic data (gen 1..N) + real data\n\nUsage:\n    python iterative_train.py \\\n        --model_name EleutherAI/pythia-410m \\\n        --data_dir data/wikitext103 \\\n argparse\nimport json\nimport os\nimport random\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom datasets import Dataset, concatenate_datasets, load_from_disk\nfrom torch.utils.data import DataLoader\nfrom transformers import (\n    AutoModelForCausalLM,\n    AutoTokenizer,\n    DataCollatorForLanguageModeling,\n    Trainer,\n    TrainingArguments,\n    set_seed,\n)\n\nfrom metrics import compute_all_metrics\n\n\ndef parse_args():\n    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    p.add_argument(\"--model_name\", type=str, default=\"EleutherAI/pythia-410m\")\n    p.add_argument(\"--data_dir\", type=str, default=\"data/wikitext103\",\n                   help=\"Output of prepare_data.py\")\n    p.add_argument(\"--dataset\", type=str, default=\"wikitext-103-v1\",\n                   help=\"Dataset identifier (for logging only; actual data from data_dir)\")\n    p.add_argument(\"--synthetic_ratio\", type=float, default=1.0,\n                   help=\"Fraction of training data that is synthetic (0-1)\")\n    p.add_argument(\"--num_generations\", type=int, default=10)\n    p.add_argument(\"--steps_per_gen\", type=int, default=5000)\n    p.add_argument(\"--output_dir\", type=str, default=\"results/mvp/rep_42\")\n    p.add_argument(\"--seed\", type=int, default=42)\n    p.add_argument(\"--batch_size\", type=int, default=4)\n    p.add_argument(\"--max_length\", type=int, default=512)\n    p.add_argument(\"--lr\", type=float, default=5e-5)\n    p.add_argument(\"--gen_max_new_tokens\", type=int, default=256,\n                   help=\"Max new tokens per generation sample\")\n    p.add_argument(\"--gen_batch_size\", type=int, default=16)\n    p.add_argument(\"--top_p\", type=float, default=0.95,\n                   help=\"Nucleus sampling p for synthetic generation\")\n    p.add_argument(\"--accumulate\", action=\"store_true\",\n                   help=\"Pool all historical synthetic data instead of only the latest generation\")\n    return p.parse_args()\n\n\ndef generate_synthetic_data(model, tokenizer, prompts_dataset, args):\n    \"\"\"Generate synthetic text from `model` using held-out prompts.\"\"\"\n    model.eval()\n    device = next(model.parameters()).device\n    generated_texts = []\n\n    prompt_texts = prompts_dataset[\"text\"]\n    for i in range(0, len(prompt_texts), args.gen_batch_size):\n        batch_prompts = prompt_texts[i:i + args.gen_batch_size]\n        enc = tokenizer(batch_prompts, return_tensors=\"pt\", truncation=True,\n                        max_length=args.max_length // 2, padding=True)\n        input_ids = enc[\"input_ids\"].to(device)\n        attention_mask = enc[\"attention_mask\"].to(device)\n\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            outputs = model.generate(\n                input_ids=input_ids,\n                attention_mask=attention_mask,\n                max_new_tokens=args.gen_max_new_tokens,\n                do_sample=True,\n                top_p=args.top_p,\n                temperature=1.0,\n                pad_token_id=tokenizer.pad_token_id or tokenizer.eos_token_id,\n            )\n        for seq in outputs:\n            text = tokenizer.decode(seq, skip_special_tokens=True)\n            generated_texts.append(text)\n\n    return Dataset.from_dict({\"text\": generated_texts})\n\n\ndef mix_datasets(real_dataset, synthetic_dataset, synthetic_ratio, seed):\n    \"\"\"Mix real and synthetic data according to synthetic_ratio.\n\n    Bidirectional subsampling to hit the target ratio:\n    - Low α (enough synthetic): keep all real, subsample synthetic\n    - High α (synthetic scarce): keep all synthetic, subsample real\n    \"\"\"\n    if synthetic_ratio >= 1.0:\n        return synthetic_dataset\n    if synthetic_ratio <= 0.0:\n        return real_dataset\n\n    needed_syn = int(len(real_dataset) * synthetic_ratio / (1 - synthetic_ratio))\n    if needed_syn <= len(synthetic_dataset):\n        syn_subset = synthetic_dataset.select(range(needed_syn))\n        real_subset = real_dataset\n    else:\n        needed_real = int(len(synthetic_dataset) * (1 - synthetic_ratio) / synthetic_ratio)\n        needed_real = min(needed_real, len(real_dataset))\n        real_subset = real_dataset.select(range(needed_real))\n        syn_subset = synthetic_dataset\n\n    actual_ratio = len(syn_subset) / (len(syn_subset) + len(real_subset))\n    print(f\"  Mix: {len(syn_subset)} syn + {len(real_subset)} real = {actual_ratio:.4f}\")\n    return concatenate_datasets([real_subset, syn_subset]).shuffle(seed=seed)\n\n\ndef tokenize_dataset(dataset, tokenizer, max_length):\n    \"\"\"Tokenize and chunk text for causal LM training.\"\"\"\n    def tokenize_fn(examples):\n        return tokenizer(examples[\"text\"], truncation=True,\n                         max_length=max_length, padding=\"max_length\")\n\n    tokenized = dataset.map(tokenize_fn, batched=True,\n                            remove_columns=[\"text\"],\n                            num_proc=1)\n    tokenized.set_format(\"torch\")\n    return tokenized\n\n\ndef train_one_generation(model, tokenizer, train_dataset, args, gen_idx):\n    \"\"\"Fine-tune model for one generation.\"\"\"\n    tokenized = tokenize_dataset(train_dataset, tokenizer, args.max_length)\n\n    training_args = TrainingArguments(\n        output_dir=os.path.join(args.output_dir, f\"gen_{gen_idx}\", \"trainer\"),\n        max_steps=args.steps_per_gen,\n        per_device_train_batch_size=args.batch_size,\n        learning_rate=args.lr,\n        warmup_steps=min(500, args.steps_per_gen // 10),\n        weight_decay=0.01,\n        logging_steps=100,\n        save_strategy=\"no\",\n        seed=args.seed + gen_idx,\n        fp16=torch.cuda.is_available(),\n        dataloader_drop_last=True,\n        report_to=\"none\",\n    )\n\n    collator = DataCollatorForLanguageModeling(tokenizer=tokenizer, mlm=False)\n    trainer = Trainer(\n        model=model,\n        args=training_args,\n        train_dataset=tokenized,\n        data_collator=collator,\n    )\n    trainer.train()\n    return model\n\n\ndef main():\n    args = parse_args()\n    set_seed(args.seed)\n    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)\n\n    with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, \"config.json\"), \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(vars(args), f, indent=2)\n\n    print(f\"Loading model: {args.model_name}\")\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model_name)\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\n\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(args.model_name)\n    if torch.cuda.is_available():\n        model = model.cuda()\n\n    print(f\"Loading data from {args.data_dir}\")\n    train_dataset = load_from_disk(os.path.join(args.data_dir, \"train\"))\n    eval_dataset = load_from_disk(os.path.join(args.data_dir, \"eval\"))\n    prompts_dataset = load_from_disk(os.path.join(args.data_dir, \"prompts\"))\n\n    all_metrics = []\n    synthetic_pool = []  # for --accumulate mode\n\n    # --- Generation 0: baseline metrics (no training yet) ---\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"Generation 0 (baseline)\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    gen0_synthetic = generate_synthetic_data(model, tokenizer, prompts_dataset, args)\n    gen0_dir = os.path.join(args.output_dir, \"gen_0\")\n    os.makedirs(gen0_dir, exist_ok=True)\n    gen0_synthetic.save_to_disk(os.path.join(gen0_dir, \"synthetic_data\"))\n\n    metrics = compute_all_metrics(model, tokenizer, eval_dataset, gen0_synthetic)\n    metrics[\"generation\"] = 0\n    all_metrics.append(metrics)\n\n    with open(os.path.join(gen0_dir, \"metrics.json\"), \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(metrics, f, indent=2)\n    print(f\"Gen 0 metrics: ppl={metrics['perplexity']:.2f}, \"\n          f\"entropy={metrics['token_entropy']:.4f}, \"\n          f\"eff_rank={metrics['effective_rank']:.1f}\")\n\n    # --- Generations 1..N ---\n    for gen in range(1, args.num_generations + 1):\n        print(\"=\" * 60)\n        print(f\"Generation {gen}/{args.num_generations}\")\n        print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n        gen_dir = os.path.join(args.output_dir, f\"gen_{gen}\")\n        os.makedirs(gen_dir, exist_ok=True)\n\n        # Step 1: generate synthetic data with current model\n        print(\"  Generating synthetic data …\")\n        synthetic_data = generate_synthetic_data(\n            model, tokenizer, prompts_dataset, args)\n        synthetic_data.save_to_disk(os.path.join(gen_dir, \"synthetic_data\"))\n\n        # Step 2: mix real + synthetic\n        if args.accumulate:\n            synthetic_pool.append(synthetic_data)\n            effective_synthetic = concatenate_datasets(synthetic_pool)\n        else:\n            effective_synthetic = synthetic_data\n        mixed = mix_datasets(train_dataset, effective_synthetic,\n                             args.synthetic_ratio, args.seed + gen)\n        print(f\"  Mixed dataset: {len(mixed)} samples \"\n              f\"(synthetic={len(effective_synthetic)}, \"\n              f\"synthetic_ratio={args.synthetic_ratio}, \"\n              f\"accumulate={args.accumulate})\")\n\n        # Step 3: fine-tune\n        print(\"  Training …\")\n        model = train_one_generation(model, tokenizer, mixed, args, gen)\n\n        # Step 4: save checkpoint\n        ckpt_path = os.path.join(gen_dir, \"checkpoint\")\n        model.save_pretrained(ckpt_path)\n        tokenizer.save_pretrained(ckpt_path)\n        print(f\"  Checkpoint saved: {ckpt_path}\")\n\n        # Step 5: compute metrics\n        print(\"  Computing metrics …\")\n        metrics = compute_all_metrics(model, tokenizer, eval_dataset,\n                                      synthetic_data)\n        metrics[\"generation\"] = gen\n        all_metrics.append(metrics)\n\n        with open(os.path.join(gen_dir, \"metrics.json\"), \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(metrics, f, indent=2)\n        print(f\"  ppl={metrics['perplexity']:.2f}, \"\n              f\"entropy={metrics['token_entropy']:.4f}, \"\n              f\"eff_rank={metrics['effective_rank']:.1f}, \"\n              f\"mauve={metrics['mauve']:.4f}\")\n\n    # Save consolidated metrics\n    metrics_path = os.path.join(args.output_dir, \"all_metrics.json\")\n    with open(metrics_path, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(all_metrics, f, indent=2)\n    print(f\"\\nAll metrics saved to {metrics_path}\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 279
      }
    },

type=int, default=10)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--steps_per_gen\\\", type=int, default=5000)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--output_dir\\\", type=str, default=\\\"results/mvp/rep_42\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--seed\\\", type=int, default=42)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--batch_size\\\", type=int, default=4)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--max_length\\\", type=int, default=512)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--lr\\\", type=float, default=5e-5)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--gen_max_new_tokens\\\", type=int, default=256,\\n                   help=\\\"Max new tokens per generation sample\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--gen_batch_size\\\", type=int, default=16)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--top_p\\\", type=float, default=0.95,\\n                   help=\\\"Nucleus sampling p for synthetic generation\\\")\\n    return p.parse_args()\\n\\n\\ndef generate_synthetic_data(model, tokenizer, prompts_dataset, args):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Generate synthetic text from `model` using held-out prompts.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    model.eval()\\n    device = next(model.parameters()).device\\n    generated_texts = []\\n\\n    prompt_texts = prompts_dataset[\\\"text\\\"]\\n    for i in range(0, len(prompt_texts), args.gen_batch_size):\\n        batch_prompts = prompt_texts[i:i + args.gen_batch_size]\\n        enc = tokenizer(batch_prompts, return_tensors=\\\"pt\\\", truncation=True,\\n with torch.no_grad():\\n            outputs = model.generate(\\n                input_ids=input_ids,\\n                attention_mask=attention_mask,\\n                max_new_tokens=args.gen_max_new_tokens,\\n                do_sample=True,\\n                top_p=args.top_p,\\n                temperature=1.0,\\n                pad_token_id=tokenizer.pad_token_id or tokenizer.eos_token_id,\\n            )\\n        for seq in outputs:\\n            text = tokenizer.decode(seq, skip_special_tokens=True)\\n            generated_texts.append(text)\\n\\n    return Dataset.from_dict({\\\"text\\\": generated_texts})\\n\\n\\ndef mix_datasets(real_dataset, synthetic_dataset, synthetic_ratio, seed):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Mix real and synthetic data according to synthetic_ratio.\\n\\n    Uses ALL synthetic data; adds proportional real data to reach the\\n    target ratio.  synthetic_ratio=1.0 → pure synthetic (full replacement).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if synthetic_ratio >= 1.0:\\n        return synthetic_dataset\\n    if synthetic_ratio <= 0.0:\\n        return real_dataset\\n\\n    n_syn = len(synthetic_dataset)\\n    n_real = int(n_syn * (1 - synthetic_ratio) / synthetic_ratio)\\n    n_real = min(n_real, len(real_dataset))\\n\\n    rng = random.Random(seed)\\n    real_indices = rng.sample(range(len(real_dataset)), n_real)\\n    real_subset = real_dataset.select(real_indices)\\n    return concatenate_datasets([real_subset, synthetic_dataset]).shuffle(seed=seed)\\n\\n\\ndef tokenize_dataset(dataset, tokenizer, max_length):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Tokenize and chunk text for causal LM training.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    def tokenize_fn(examples):\\n        return tokenizer(examples[\\\"text\\\"], truncation=True,\\n                         max_length=max_length, padding=\\\"max_length\\\")\\n\\n    tokenized = dataset.map(tokenize_fn, batched=True,\\n                            remove_columns=[\\\"text\\\"],\\n                            num_proc=1)\\n    tokenized.set_format(\\\"torch\\\")\\n    return tokenized\\n\\n\\ndef train_one_generation(model, tokenizer, train_dataset, args, gen_idx):\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": Let me parse the key findings from the 4-seed analysis:

**Verdict: STRONG PASS** — 2 canary families (ECE, token_entropy) each have ≥1 SIGNIFICANT LEAD pair.

**Significant Lead Pairs (6):**
1. token_entropy → distinct_1: lag=-1 (all 4 seeds), p < 0.004 all seeds, |r| > 0.99
2. token_entropy → distinct_2: lag=-1 (3 seeds) / -2 (seed 44), p < 0.024, lag_std=0.43


3. token_entropy → distinct_3: lag varies across seeds with std=1.64, previously flagged as unstable but now meets significance threshold
4. ECE → distinct_1: checking now
5. ECE → distinct_2: checking now
6. ECE → distinct_3: checking now

**Weak Lead Pairs (1):**
- token_entropy → perplexity: lag=-5 (all 4 seeds), p ≈ 0.06-0.12 — not significant

**Key observations:**
- Looking at the broader patterns...

UVE got demoted due to floor effects, and effective_rank shows conflicting slopes across seeds—two positive, two negative—which suggests no consistent directional relationship. I need to verify the analysis actually used all 4 seeds, check that the per-seed data is internally consistent, confirm the verdict logic matches the data, and review whether the methodology is sound. The token_entropy → distinct_3 relationship has a lag standard deviation of 1.64, which falls below the high dispersion threshold of 2.0, so it shouldn't be classified as unstable based on that criterion alone.

However, seed 42's lag of -5 is a significant outlier compared to the other seeds clustering at -1 or -2. In the 2-seed analysis this pair was flagged as unstable with a lag_std of 2.0, but adding seeds 43, 44, and 45 brought the overall standard deviation down to 1.64 just because they happened to cluster together. That statistical improvement masks the fact that seed 42 remains a 4-generation outlier from the mode, which warrants closer scrutiny.

Looking at the individual results for token_entropy → distinct_3, all four seeds show significant p-values below 0.05, which is encouraging. But the lag values range from -1 to -5 across seeds, making distinct_3 a less reliable leading indicator compared to distinct_1 (which consistently lags by -1) or distinct_2 (which stays between -1 and -2).

The real problem is effective_rank — the slopes across seeds are +0.81, -1.90, -2.11, and +1.56, which show no consistent directional pattern. Across all downstream pairs, effective_rank shows either no lead or only weak lead relationships, meaning it's not functioning as a useful early warning signal in the 100% SC regime.

This finding highlights how much the results depend on which seeds are selected. Rep44 is now marked complete with its metrics populated, though the legacy mvp_rep44 entry from the earlier failed attempt is still sitting in PREPARING status. Looking at the ECE results, the output got cut off, but from the significant_lead_pairs list I can see that ECE shows significant lead relationships with distinct_1, distinct_2, and distinct_3. I should check the lead_time_table.csv to get a cleaner summary of all these relationships.

Generating metrics table... Looking at the correlation metrics across different model evaluation dimensions, I'm seeing some interesting patterns emerge—particularly how effective_rank shows strong statistical significance with distinct_3 while most other metric pairs don't reach that threshold.

The key insight here is that ECE's relationship with perplexity is actually lagging, not leading—it follows perplexity degradation rather than predicting it, which contradicts what the initial analysis suggested. Meanwhile, token_entropy does show a consistent negative lag of -5 across all seeds, though the p-values hover just above the significance threshold, making it a borderline case for a true leading indicator.

Looking at the other metrics, effective_rank shows no consistent pattern across seeds with slopes splitting evenly, so it's not reliable as a canary. Self_bleu is similarly unreliable with high variance in lag estimates across runs. This means I'm down to just two genuinely useful leading indicators—ECE and token_entropy—rather than the four canary families I initially thought I had.

The good news is that both token_entropy and ECE consistently lead distinct_1 and distinct_2 by about one generation, with token_entropy showing perfect consistency. Token_entropy's relationship with perplexity is weaker though, with a lag of -5 that might extend beyond my test window.

Now I'm verifying the raw data from seed 44 to make sure everything checks out — the generation 0 metrics match across seeds, and the registry values for generation 10 align with what I'm seeing in the data. The effective_rank trajectory for this seed shows it starting around 46.6, peaking at 105.9 around generation 6, then settling back down to 88 by generation 10.

Looking at the agent's conclusion of "STRONG PASS," I need to think through what this actually means. The reasoning is technically sound — both ECE and token_entropy do show significant lead pairs over the diversity metrics. But I should be precise about what's really happening here: the canaries are predicting diversity collapse, not perplexity degradation. Only 2 out of 4 canary families actually work as predictors, since effective_rank and self_bleu don't show the expected patterns. The "predictability boundary" framing still holds though — some dimensions are predictable while others aren't. I also need to check whether the analysis properly accounts for those negative correlations I'm seeing with effective_rank in some pairs.

Looking at seed 44's data, effective_rank climbs from 46.6 up to 105.9, then drops back down to 88.1, while distinct_1 starts at 0.098 and quickly settles at 0.073. The issue is that effective_rank's non-monotonic behavior makes it unreliable as a predictor — it goes up while distinct goes down initially, but then effective_rank also decreases while distinct stays flat. This inconsistency is what makes the correlation inconclusive.

Now compiling my review. The STRONG PASS verdict is technically justified with 6 significant lead pairs, but I need to flag several critical issues: the canary-to-perplexity pathway has real problems with token_entropy showing weak significance and ECE moving in the wrong direction with a 4.75 lag. Two of the four canary families are problematic — effective_rank is inconclusive and self_bleu is inconsistent — so the paper needs to be transparent about these failures. The token_entropy to distinct_3 lag shows concerning variance across seeds, with seed 42 as a notable outlier at -5 despite the overall pattern being promoted to significance. On the positive side, the rep44 registry now correctly reflects success, resolving a previous critical issue, and I've verified the raw data for rep44 generation 0 metrics.

Now I'm checking whether the cross-seed baseline matches and if generation 10 aligns with the registry, then looking for any conclusion or report the worker generated.

论文必须诚实报告：4 个候选 canary 中仅 2 个有效，另 2 个在 100% SC 下无预测力。\n\n- [severity: warning] **token_entropy→distinct_3 仍有 seed 42 异常**：lag 分布为 {-5, -1, -2, -1}，seed 42 偏离其余 seeds 4 个 generation。4-seed 后 lag_std=1.64（降到 high_dispersion 阈值以下），被分析脚本提升为 SIGNIFICANT。但从原始数据看，distinct_3 的领先性不如 distinct_1/2 可靠。论文对 distinct_3 的 claim 强度应弱于 distinct_1/2。\n\n- [severity: warning] **token_entropy→perplexity 的 lag=-5 可能是窗口截断伪影**。分析脚本最大允许 lag=±5，4 seeds 全部命中 lag=-5 上界。真实峰值可能在 lag<-5 处（即 >5 generation 的领先时间）。这不影响\"存在领先\"的结论，但影响 lead time 的精确量化。如果 alpha-sweep 的时间序列更短（例如 10 gen × partial collapse），此截断问题更严重。\n\n- [severity: info] **report.md 过时**。仍显示 rep44=failed，未反映 4-seed 分析完成。建议更新。\n\n### 对论文 framing 的影响\n\n4-seed 结果支持\"predictability boundary\"框架：\n- ✅ 可预测：diversity 退化（token_entropy/ECE → distinct_1/2/3，1-2 gen 领先）\n- ❌ 不可预测：perplexity 爆炸（canary 无显著领先或方向错误）\n- ❌ 不可预测：representation collapse（effective_rank 自身方向不一致）\n\n这比\"canary 预测 collapse\"更精细也更诚实——正好契合 phase diagram 框架。但 Agent 叙事中需要明确这个边界，不能模糊处理。\n\n### 补实验建议\n\n无新增补实验需求。当前数据足以支撑 MVP 结论，alpha-sweep 按计划推进即可。但提醒：\n1. alpha-sweep 的 analyze_mvp.py 应增大 max_lag 参数（建议 ≥7），避免 lag=-5 截断伪影\n2. 远程 iterative_train.py 尚未部署双向修复——alpha-sweep 仍被阻塞",

The GPU budget totals around 92 hours across priorities, which splits to roughly 46 hours per card on dual hardware.

With two cards running ~12 hours daily, I'm looking at about 4 days of compute time, and given the AAAI deadline is roughly 10 weeks away, there's comfortable headroom. I notice the roadmap is missing explicit mention of test_alpha_sweep.py for pre-flight validation and doesn't list those missing α values, so I should flag those gaps.

Looking at the decisions table, D008 about distinct_3 needs updating—the 4-seed results show the lag distribution is {-5, -1, -2, -1}, which is clearly bimodal with seed 42 as an outlier, so the decision criteria should reflect that.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the complete picture. Let me analyze the ROADMAP and experiment plan:

**Experiment Plan Overview:**
- 8 experiments total
- P0: plan_001 (α-sweep, 30h GPU), plan_002 (method ablation, 0h GPU), plan_003 (MVP formalization, 0h GPU)
- P1: plan_004 (C4 cross-domain, 10h), plan_005 (1.4B scale, 24h), plan_006 (extended 20-gen run, 8h)


- P2: plan_007 (CRS intervention, 10h), plan_008 (accumulate ablation, 10h)

Looking at the α-sweep in plan_001, I notice it's missing critical boundary conditions—specifically α=0.0 to establish a clean baseline where the canary signal should disappear entirely, and α=0.90 to characterize the high-contamination regime before hitting saturation at α=1.0. Without these endpoints, the phase diagram lacks the empirical anchors needed to validate the theoretical predictions.

For plan_003, the "orphaned" status is puzzling since the 4-seed analysis already completed and updated analysis_summary.json, but the comma-separated registry format might be causing a mismatch with the registry system. The max_lag=7 reanalysis portion still needs to be executed to finish that plan.

Decision D008 should actually trigger based on the 4-seed results—token_entropy→distinct_3 shows bimodal lag values across seeds (seed 42 diverges at -5 while others cluster at -1/-2)—so the "降级" action needs to be reflected in the roadmap. GPU allocation looks solid at 92 hours across 2 cards with no timeline pressure, and the dependency structure between plans seems sound. Plan_006's extended 20-generation approach directly tackles the lag concerns I had.

The real priorities I'm seeing are checking whether α=0.0 is missing from the sweep as a baseline, clarifying plan_003's orphaned status, and making sure D008's outcome actually maps to the execution plan. I should also verify the claim mappings against paper_framing.md before posting my review.
```

distrib_canary_collapse\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: AAAI 2027 (expected deadline ~August 2026)\\n- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings (ARR October cycle if AAAI timeline tight)\\n- Note: EMNLP 2026 Main ARR May deadline已过；AAAI 2027 给出充足实验时间\\n\\n## Core Claims (3 条)\\n\\n1. **Canary→Diversity Lead**: 在迭代合成数据训练中，ECE 和 token entropy 作为廉价 canary 指标，稳定领先 diversity 退化（distinct_1/2）1-2 代——提供可量化的早期预警窗口。\\n2. **Regime-Dependent Predictability**: 可预测性取决于 contamination regime——100% self-consuming 下 perplexity 与 canary 同步崩溃（不可预测），低 α 条件下 collapse 更渐进、canary lead time 有望扩展到 quality 指标。Predictability phase diagram 划定可预测/不可预测边界。\\n3. **Methodological Rigor**: 朴素相关分析在非平稳训练指标上产生伪回归；Toda-Yamamoto panel temporal precedence 提供统计严谨的 lead-lag 量化，方法论 ablation 量化伪显著关联被推翻的比例。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Model collapse 的在线监控工具已存在（SIGMA 谱监控、DCES 反应式干预），但没有工作系统性回答\\\"哪些指标最早预测崩溃、领先多久、在什么 regime 下有效\\\"。Du et al. 非正式观察到 ECE 领先 accuracy，但仅限单指标对、1-3 代、无统计检验。\\n\\n- **Existing approaches & their gaps**: SIGMA 提供谱监控但无 lead-time 量化；DCES 反应式而非预测式；Du et al. 是最接近的经验前辈但方法论不足（无正式 time-series 检验、无多家族对比）；Kalinowski 拓扑 CI 针对表征坍缩非合成数据退化且计算昂贵。Truong & Truong 证明一阶相变下标准早期预警指标失效——但仅限特定 regime 和特定指标族。\\n\\n- **Our framing**: 我们不是提出\\\"通用 canary\\\"，而是**系统性映射可预测性的边界**——哪些 collapse 维度可预测（diversity）、哪些不可预测（perplexity in extreme regime）、在什么 contamination 条件下、用什么统计方法。Null results（canary 不 lead perplexity）不是失败，而是 phase diagram 中的一个 boundary data point。\\n\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"ECE and token entropy predict diversity collapse 1-2 generations ahead (|r|>0.99, p<0.001, 4/4 seeds) in iterative synthetic data training, while a predictability phase diagram reveals regime-dependent boundaries where canary signals exist vs. where synchronous collapse precludes early warning.\\\"\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\n\\n| Concern | Response Strategy | Required Experiment |\\n|---------|-------------------|---------------------|\\n| \\\"Canary 不预测 perplexity，实用价值有限\\\" | Framing 为 regime-dependent：100% SC 是极端 regime；α-sweep 展示低 α 下 canary→perplexity 是否恢复 | α-sweep (P0) |\\n| \\\"只在 Pythia-410M 上验证，scale 泛化？\\\" | Spot-check Pythia-1.4B × 核心条件 | scale-check (P1) |\\n| \\\"WikiText-103 单一数据集\\\" | C4 cross-domain validation | domain-transfer (P1) |\\n| \\\"为什么不用简单相关分析？\\\" | 方法 ablation：naive corr vs Toda-Yamamoto，量化伪显著比例 | method-ablation (P0, 零 GPU) |\\n| \\\"4 个 canary 中 2 个失败，选择偏差？\\\" | 全量报告 4 族结果，诚实讨论 effective_rank/self_bleu 失败原因 | 已有 MVP 数据 |\\n| \\\"Intervention 实验缺失\\\" | CRS-triggered vs reactive vs no-intervention 对比 | intervention (P2) |\\n\\n## Venue Fit\\n\\nAAAI 2027 优势：\\n- ML 方法论 track 接受 empirical analysis + 统计方法论贡献\\n- 不要求 SOTA，接受 systematic study with novel methodology\\n- Phase diagram / regime boundary 概念在 AAAI 偏理论的审稿人中有吸引力\\n\\n风险：\\n- 如果被认为\\\"只是 empirical study 没有算法贡献\\\"→ 补 CRS + intervention 提升 method 贡献\\n- 模型规模偏小（Pythia-410M/1.4B）→ 诚实标注 limitation + scaling trend\\n\\n## Key Narrative Constraints (from MVP)\\n\\n1. **不能 claim \\\"canary predicts model collapse\\\"** — 只能 claim \\\"canary predicts diversity collapse\\\"，perplexity 通道在 100% SC 下断裂\\n2. **effective_rank 和 self_bleu 作为 canary 失败** — 必须全量报告，不能只报正面结果\\n3. **distinct_3 的 lead time 不如 distinct_1/2 可靠** — 主文聚焦 distinct_1/2，distinct_3 放 supplementary\\n4. **max_lag=5 可能截断真实 lead** — α-sweep 中增大 max_lag≥7\\n\", \"total_chars\": 3175, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The paper_framing.md has been updated to reflect my review findings. Let me check:

1. **Key Narrative Constraints** — all 4 match my 4-seed review findings:
   - ✅ Cannot claim "canary predicts model collapse" — only diversity collapse
   - ✅ effective_rank and self_bleu fail — must report fully
   - ✅ distinct_3 less reliable — supplementary
   - ✅ max_lag=5 truncation concern — increase in α-sweep

2. **Core Claims** map to experiment plan claims:
   - Claim 1: Canary→Diversity Lead (plan_003, plan_006)
   - Claim 2: Regime-Dependent Predictability (plan_001, plan_004, plan_005, plan_006, plan_007)
   - Claim 3: Methodological Rigor (plan_002)

3. **Reviewer Concerns** are well anticipated.

Now I'm finalizing the ROADMAP review and spotting a critical gap: plan_001's α-sweep covers {0.25, 0.50, 0.75} while the MVP uses α=1.0, but there's no α=0.0 baseline. For a proper "predictability phase diagram" supporting Claim 2, I need to include the zero-weighting condition to show the full spectrum.

At α=0.0, there's no synthetic data mixed in at all, so the canary metrics should remain stable under repeated retraining on real data alone—this serves as the essential control condition. Without it, reviewers could dismiss any canary signal as a training artifact from repeated finetuning rather than evidence of synthetic contamination. Running this for 10 generations would definitively show that pure real-data retraining doesn't produce the signal, which directly addresses anticipated concerns about selection bias and strengthens Claim 2.

I'm also noticing plan_003 appears orphaned and needs clarification on its status.

The α=0.90 point is missing from the sweep, which is a gap since it would test the behavior when only 10% real data is added—bridging the phase diagram between α=0.75 and the MVP's α=1.0. The GPU budget constraints probably drove that decision, but it's worth noting what we're trading off.

For D008, the 4-seed results confirm distinct_3 lag shows bimodal behavior across seeds, which aligns with how paper_framing.md already handles it by moving that constraint to supplementary material.

On the pending "dream" item mentioned in the roadmap, I'm not sure what that refers to, so I'll flag it but can't assess it further.

Looking at the overall picture, the roadmap captures the MVP findings well, the narrative constraints directly address my earlier feedback, the GPU budget works for the timeline, and the experiment structure is solid with clear priorities. The team has also anticipated reviewer concerns effectively. Time to write this up.
```

**格式硬约束**:
- 整条回复**只能**是 &lt;handoff>...&lt;/handoff> 标签包裹

**必须覆盖以下 5 个 section（缺一不可，无内容写 N/A）**:

### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
  本项目特定的不可违反约束（GPU 设备号、服务器名、账号、路径限制等）。
  写本项目特定的，不要抄 CLAUDE.md 通用 L1 红线。
  下一个"你"第一眼看到的就是这里，写错了会被 Director CRITICAL。

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
  - 服务器名 + 工作目录 + Python 路径 + conda env
  - 模型路径 / 数据集路径 / wandb config / HF cache
  - 活跃进程：PID + tmux session + cron 任务 ID
  写一次就稳定，下一个"你"不用再 SSH 探查。

### 3. 📊 当前状态
  - 你刚做完什么 / 正在做什么
  - registry 里 RUNNING/PREPARING 的实验列表 + 一句话摘要
  - 等待中的 Worker 回调 / Cron / Director 决策

### 4. 📋 待办（条件触发式）
  格式 "条件 X 满足 → 动作 Y"。例如:
  - "Falcon-H1R 下载完成 → sed 修 MODEL_PATH → dry-run → 提交"
  - "pilot_011 出 GC4 结果 → ≥2pp 转架构 narrative，<2pp 写 null-result"

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
  - 项目核心结论 / 决策链（D001..）
  - 下一个"你"最容易判断错的事 / 容易踩的坑

**写作原则**：
- 第 1 节必须是项目特定的（不是通用 L1 红线）
- 第 2 节让下一轮 BOOT 后能立即开工，避免重新 SSH 探查
- 简洁 > 完整：每节 5-10 行足够，长 handoff 不等于好 handoff

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照

- Python：/root/miniconda3/bin/python（绝对路径，不走 conda activate）
- 模型：/root/autodl-tmp/qwen35_4b_direct
- 活跃：pilot_011 (PID 203221, tmux=pilot_011, cuda:4)

我刚完成 pilot_010 分析，正在等 pilot_011 (Falcon-H1R) CoT 生成完成（6/50）。
